const express = require('express'); const cors = require('cors'); let compression; try { compression = require('compression'); } catch (e) { console.warn('compression package not installed — responses will not be gzipped. Run: npm install compression'); compression = null; } const oracledb = require('oracledb'); const fs = require('fs'); const path = require('path'); const crypto = require('crypto'); const AdmZip = require('adm-zip'); const bcrypt = require('bcryptjs'); const jwt = require('jsonwebtoken'); const PORTAL_PUBLIC_BASE_URL = (process.env.WHATSAPP_PUBLIC_BASE_URL || process.env.RENDER_EXTERNAL_URL || 'https://hallan-picking-backend.onrender.com').replace(/\/$/, ''); // ---- Restaurant order XML export (pull-based) ---- // Render can't reach the shop's local network directly (no port forwarding // set up, and we deliberately avoided it for security). So instead of // Render pushing files via FTP, a small script running ON the shop PC // (order-xml-puller.js, separate file) polls the endpoint below every // couple of minutes, asks "what's new", and writes the XML files onto its // own local disk — where FileZilla Server/EPOS then picks them up exactly // as already configured. This shared secret is just so that endpoint can't // be called by randoms on the internet; set the same value in // LOCAL_SYNC_SECRET here and in order-xml-puller.js on the shop PC. const LOCAL_SYNC_SECRET = process.env.LOCAL_SYNC_SECRET || ''; function xmlEscape(v) { return String(v === undefined || v === null ? '' : v) .replace(/&/g, '&') .replace(//g, '>') .replace(/"/g, '"') .replace(/'/g, '''); } // Builds the XML document for one submitted restaurant order. function buildOrderXml(orderId, customerId, customerName, items) { const itemsXml = items.map(it => ` ${xmlEscape(it.stockid || '')} ${xmlEscape(it.item_name)} ${xmlEscape(it.qty)} ${xmlEscape(it.trade_price)} ${xmlEscape(it.source || 'OTHER')} ${xmlEscape(it.notes || '')} `).join('\n'); return ` ${xmlEscape(orderId)} ${xmlEscape(customerId)} ${xmlEscape(customerName)} ${xmlEscape(new Date().toISOString())} ${itemsXml} `; } function requireLocalSyncSecret(req, res, next) { const provided = req.headers['x-sync-secret'] || ''; if (!LOCAL_SYNC_SECRET || provided !== LOCAL_SYNC_SECRET) { return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Invalid or missing sync secret' }); } next(); } /* --------------------------------------------------------- TIME ZONE: everything in this app (shelf replenishment timestamps in particular) is UK time. Pinning process.env.TZ here — instead of trusting whatever timezone the host happens to be set to — makes every `new Date()` / date formatting call in this file behave as UK wall-clock time (GMT in winter, BST in summer), consistently, regardless of the server's own default. This must be set before anything else touches Date/Oracle. --------------------------------------------------------- */ process.env.TZ = 'Europe/London'; const app = express(); // Render sits in front of this app as a reverse proxy, so without this, // req.ip always resolves to Render's internal proxy address instead of the // real visitor's IP — which would silently break the IP allowlist below. app.set('trust proxy', 1); app.use(cors()); // Gzip every response — index.html alone is 200KB+ of inline JS, and every // JSON list (stock search, print data, HR records...) benefits. This is a // pure network-transfer-time win and costs nothing on the DB side. if (compression) app.use(compression()); // Body size limit. A single 140mb limit applies to every route, globally. // The webhook gets its own tiny parser that also keeps the raw bytes, // needed to verify Meta's signature (see requireWhatsappSignature below). const jsonParserLarge = express.json({ limit: '140mb' }); const jsonParserWebhook = express.json({ limit: '1mb', verify: (req, res, buf) => { req.rawBody = buf; } }); app.use((req, res, next) => { const normalizedPath = req.path.length > 1 && req.path.endsWith('/') ? req.path.slice(0, -1) : req.path; if (normalizedPath === '/api/whatsapp/webhook') return jsonParserWebhook(req, res, next); return jsonParserLarge(req, res, next); }); // Serves the staff portal's static frontend (rewired index.html) at /staff-portal/ // maxAge caches genuinely static files (images, fonts, etc.) in the browser so // they aren't re-fetched on every page load; index.html itself is excluded // (setHeaders below) since it changes whenever you redeploy and staff should // always get the latest version, not a stale cached copy. app.use('/staff-portal', express.static(path.join(__dirname, 'staff-portal-frontend'), { maxAge: '1d', setHeaders: (res, filePath) => { if (filePath.endsWith('.html')) res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-cache'); } })); // Serves the standalone WhatsApp Embedded Signup / coexistence connect page // at /whatsapp-connect/ — a one-off setup page, not part of the main portal, // used once to link a phone number for coexistence with the Cloud API. app.use('/whatsapp-connect', express.static(path.join(__dirname, 'whatsapp-connect-frontend'), { maxAge: '1d', setHeaders: (res, filePath) => { if (filePath.endsWith('.html')) res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-cache'); } })); const PORT = process.env.PORT || 3000; if (!process.env.JWT_SECRET) { console.error('FATAL: JWT_SECRET is not set. Refusing to start — a missing secret used to fall back to a hardcoded string, which would let anyone forge an admin/management/staff token.'); process.exit(1); } const JWT_SECRET = process.env.JWT_SECRET; // Staff Portal (Holiday Portal) logins are meant to stay signed in on a // device until someone actually taps Sign Out — same reasoning as // PORTAL_TOKEN_TTL below. The JWT itself is what would otherwise force a // re-login every 12h no matter what the browser had stored, so it's now // long-lived; signing out still clears the token client-side, which is the // real logout path. const TOKEN_TTL = '3650d'; // ~10 years — "until they log out" // Portal (picking app) logins are meant to stay signed in on a device until // someone actually taps Log Out — closing the tab, rebooting, or coming back // days later shouldn't put a sign-in screen in the way of the shop floor. // The token itself is what expires, so a 12h TTL was forcing a fresh email // code twice a day no matter what the browser had stored. Portal tokens now // last effectively forever; signing out still clears the token on the device, // which is the real logout path. const PORTAL_TOKEN_TTL = '3650d'; // ~10 years — "until they log out" // The Time Clock kiosk (clockin.html) runs on a shared Android device that // is never meant to be signed out of except deliberately — asking whoever // is clocking in that moment to re-enter a 6-digit email code every 12h // isn't workable on a device nobody "owns". Employee IDs listed here get a // far longer-lived token instead of the normal 12h one; every other // account (including HR/manager logins used on the same device) is // unaffected. Add more IDs here (comma-separated) if a second kiosk device // is set up later. const KIOSK_EMPLOYEE_IDS = new Set(['2']); const KIOSK_TOKEN_TTL = '3650d'; // ~10 years — effectively "until manually logged out" /* --------------------------------------------------------- STEP 1: Unpack the wallet (sent as base64 env var) to disk --------------------------------------------------------- */ const WALLET_DIR = path.join('/tmp', 'wallet'); function setupWallet() { if (fs.existsSync(WALLET_DIR)) return; // already unpacked (warm instance) const b64 = process.env.WALLET_ZIP_BASE64; if (!b64) { console.error('WALLET_ZIP_BASE64 environment variable is not set!'); return; } fs.mkdirSync(WALLET_DIR, { recursive: true }); const zipPath = path.join('/tmp', 'wallet.zip'); fs.writeFileSync(zipPath, Buffer.from(b64, 'base64')); const zip = new AdmZip(zipPath); zip.extractAllTo(WALLET_DIR, true); console.log('Wallet unpacked to', WALLET_DIR); } setupWallet(); /* --------------------------------------------------------- STEP 2: Create a connection pool at startup --------------------------------------------------------- */ let pool; async function initPool() { pool = await oracledb.createPool({ user: process.env.DB_USER, password: process.env.DB_PASSWORD, connectString: process.env.DB_CONNECT_STRING, // e.g. hallandatabase_high configDir: WALLET_DIR, walletLocation: WALLET_DIR, walletPassword: process.env.WALLET_PASSWORD, // Was poolMin:1/poolMax:4 with no statement caching — under a handful of // staff hitting the portal at once, every request past the 4th just queued // behind whichever query was running, and every query was reparsed from // scratch. Raising the ceiling and turning on stmtCacheSize (this app // reuses the same SQL text constantly — the search/location/replenishment // endpoints in particular — so Oracle can skip reparsing and reuse the // cached execution plan) are both safe, no-schema-change wins. poolMin: 2, poolMax: 15, poolIncrement: 1, poolTimeout: 60, // seconds an idle extra connection is kept open before being closed queueTimeout: 15000, // ms a request waits for a free connection before failing loudly instead of hanging stmtCacheSize: 40 }); console.log('Oracle connection pool created'); } oracledb.outFormat = oracledb.OUT_FORMAT_OBJECT; // BLOB columns (currently only STOCK_IMAGES.IMAGEDATA, for Restaurant Order // product photos) come back as a plain Buffer instead of a Lob stream — // simpler to serve straight out to an tag. oracledb.fetchAsBuffer = [oracledb.BLOB]; async function withConnection(fn) { const conn = await pool.getConnection(); try { return await fn(conn); } finally { await conn.close(); } } function lower(rows) { // convert column names to lowercase to match frontend expectations return rows.map(r => { const out = {}; for (const k in r) out[k.toLowerCase()] = r[k]; return out; }); } /* --------------------------------------------------------- OUTBOUND EMAIL (used by the Wholesale Pricing schedule below to send status updates once a scheduled CSV run finishes, since a scheduled run has nobody watching the screen the way a manual "Download CSV" click does). Sends over Brevo's HTTPS Transactional Email API (https://api.brevo.com) rather than raw SMTP. This is a deliberate choice, not a style preference: Render blocks ALL outbound SMTP traffic (ports 25/465/587) on free-tier services as of 26 Sep 2025 — that applies to Gmail, Brevo's own SMTP relay, or any other SMTP provider equally, so no SMTP host/port combination can ever work there. An HTTPS API call on port 443 sidesteps that block entirely, since 443 is never restricted. nodemailer is no longer used for this — Node 18+ (see package.json "engines") has fetch() built in, so no extra dependency is needed. Configure via environment variables — nothing is hardcoded: BREVO_API_KEY — from Brevo → Settings → SMTP & API → API Keys MAIL_FROM — the "from" address (must be a verified sender in Brevo — Settings → Senders, Domains & Dedicated IPs) MAIL_FROM_NAME — optional display name, e.g. "Hallan Wholesale Pricing" If these aren't set, sendMail() logs a warning and resolves without throwing — a schedule still runs and updates its own status/history in the app, it just can't email out until Brevo is configured. --------------------------------------------------------- */ async function sendMail({ to, subject, text, html }) { const apiKey = process.env.BREVO_API_KEY; const fromEmail = process.env.MAIL_FROM; if (!apiKey || !fromEmail) { console.warn('sendMail skipped (BREVO_API_KEY / MAIL_FROM not configured):', subject, '->', to); return { sent: false, reason: 'BREVO_API_KEY / MAIL_FROM not configured' }; } const recipients = (Array.isArray(to) ? to : [to]).map(email => ({ email })); const resp = await fetch('https://api.brevo.com/v3/smtp/email', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'accept': 'application/json', 'content-type': 'application/json', 'api-key': apiKey }, body: JSON.stringify({ sender: { email: fromEmail, name: process.env.MAIL_FROM_NAME || 'Hallan Wholesale Pricing' }, to: recipients, subject, textContent: text, htmlContent: html || `

${String(text || '').replace(/\n/g, '
')}

` }) }); if (!resp.ok) { const body = await resp.text().catch(() => ''); throw new Error(`Brevo API error ${resp.status}: ${body.slice(0, 500)}`); } return { sent: true }; } function sleep(ms) { return new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, ms)); } function isValidEmail(email) { return typeof email === 'string' && /^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$/.test(email.trim()); } /* --------------------------------------------------------- Lenient search helpers: split into words, match any order, tolerate small spelling differences (e.g. "masoori" vs "masroori") --------------------------------------------------------- */ function levenshtein(a, b) { const m = a.length, n = b.length; const dp = Array.from({ length: m + 1 }, (_, i) => [i, ...Array(n).fill(0)]); for (let j = 0; j <= n; j++) dp[0][j] = j; for (let i = 1; i <= m; i++) { for (let j = 1; j <= n; j++) { dp[i][j] = a[i - 1] === b[j - 1] ? dp[i - 1][j - 1] : 1 + Math.min(dp[i - 1][j - 1], dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]); } } return dp[m][n]; } function wordFuzzyMatches(queryWord, nameWords) { // exact substring match (fast path) or small edit-distance match against any word in the name const qLower = queryWord.toLowerCase(); const maxEdits = qLower.length <= 3 ? 0 : qLower.length <= 6 ? 1 : 2; for (const nw of nameWords) { if (nw.includes(qLower) || qLower.includes(nw)) return true; if (levenshtein(qLower, nw) <= maxEdits) return true; } return false; } function scoreMatch(query, name) { const queryWords = query.trim().toLowerCase().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean); const nameWords = name.toLowerCase().split(/[^a-z0-9]+/).filter(Boolean); let matchedCount = 0; let totalDistance = 0; for (const qw of queryWords) { // Previously: `if (!wordFuzzyMatches(...)) return null` — every single // query word had to fuzzy-match something in the name, or the product // was dropped from results entirely. That meant one extra/misphrased // word (a size, a brand qualifier, plural vs singular) hid the exact // product staff were looking for. Now: count how many words matched // instead of requiring all of them, and only drop items where NOTHING // in the query matched anything in the name. if (!wordFuzzyMatches(qw, nameWords)) continue; matchedCount++; let best = Infinity; for (const nw of nameWords) best = Math.min(best, levenshtein(qw, nw)); totalDistance += best; } if (matchedCount === 0) return null; // nothing in the query matched anything in this name // Rank fully-matching names above partial ones, then by tightness of match. const unmatchedPenalty = (queryWords.length - matchedCount) * 5; return { matchedCount, distance: totalDistance + unmatchedPenalty }; } /* =========================================================== PORTAL LOGIN + IP ALLOWLIST + PER-SECTION PERMISSIONS Replaces the old single shared "site password" (checked only in the browser, in index.html) with real per-person passcodes issued by an admin, checked here on the server, plus an IP allowlist (also server-side — a leaked passcode alone is no longer enough) and per-section (module) access so a given staff passcode can be limited to just the sections an admin ticks for them. Requires two new Oracle tables — run this once (e.g. via DBeaver): CREATE TABLE PORTAL_USERS ( USER_ID NUMBER GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY, NAME VARCHAR2(100) NOT NULL, EMAIL VARCHAR2(200) NOT NULL, -- used for the 2FA code on every login PASSCODE_HASH VARCHAR2(200) NOT NULL, ROLE VARCHAR2(20) DEFAULT 'staff' NOT NULL, -- 'admin' or 'staff' MODULES VARCHAR2(500), -- comma-separated module keys; ignored for admin (admin = everything) ACTIVE NUMBER(1) DEFAULT 1 NOT NULL, CREATED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP ); CREATE TABLE PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS ( IP_ID NUMBER GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY, IP_ADDRESS VARCHAR2(45) NOT NULL, LABEL VARCHAR2(100), ADDED_BY VARCHAR2(100), CREATED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP ); -- Holds the one-time 2FA code between step 1 (username+passcode) and -- step 2 (code entry) of login. A user only ever has one *live* row — -- each new login attempt deletes any previous row for that user first. CREATE TABLE PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP ( OTP_ID NUMBER GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY, USER_ID NUMBER NOT NULL, CODE_HASH VARCHAR2(200) NOT NULL, EXPIRES_AT TIMESTAMP NOT NULL, ATTEMPTS NUMBER(2) DEFAULT 0 NOT NULL, CREATED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP ); ALREADY HAVE PORTAL_USERS WITHOUT AN EMAIL COLUMN? Run this once instead of recreating the table (fill in a real email per existing user afterwards — NAME here must match exactly): ALTER TABLE PORTAL_USERS ADD (EMAIL VARCHAR2(200)); UPDATE PORTAL_USERS SET EMAIL = 'shyamanth@yourdomain.com' WHERE NAME = 'Shyamanth'; COMMIT; ALTER TABLE PORTAL_USERS MODIFY (EMAIL NOT NULL); Create your own first admin row once these tables exist, e.g.: -- passcode here is a PLACEHOLDER — you must replace :hash with a real -- bcrypt hash (10 rounds) of your chosen passcode; easiest is to run -- `node -e "console.log(require('bcryptjs').hashSync('YOUR-PASSCODE',10))"` -- locally and paste the output in below. INSERT INTO PORTAL_USERS (NAME, EMAIL, PASSCODE_HASH, ROLE, MODULES, ACTIVE) VALUES ('Shyamanth', 'shyamanth@yourdomain.com', '', 'admin', NULL, 1); COMMIT; =========================================================== */ // The sections shown as tiles on the home screen. 'key' is what gets stored // in PORTAL_USERS.MODULES and checked against on every API call; 'label' is // only used to render checkboxes in Manage Users. Warehouse List, Retail // List and Making List share one 'picking' key below because they share // the same underlying /api/retail/* endpoints server-side — ticking // "Picking Lists" grants all three together. const PORTAL_MODULES = [ { key: 'picking', label: 'Picking Lists (Warehouse / Retail / Making)' }, { key: 'wholesale', label: 'Wholesale Pricing' }, { key: 'location', label: 'Location Management' }, { key: 'replenishment', label: 'Shelf Replenishment' }, { key: 'stockinfo', label: 'Stock Info' }, { key: 'supplier', label: 'Suppliers (Supplier Orders / Credit Notes)' }, { key: 'invoices', label: 'Invoices' }, { key: 'restaurant', label: 'Restaurant Orders' }, { key: 'whatsapp', label: 'WhatsApp Broadcast' }, { key: 'returns', label: 'Returns' }, { key: 'labels', label: 'Label Printer' } ]; // IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses (::ffff:203.0.113.4) show up when Node's under // certain proxy configs — strip the prefix so what's stored/compared/shown // to the admin is the plain IPv4 form they'd expect from "what's my IP". // // req.ip alone isn't reliable here: Express's "trust proxy" setting only // trusts a fixed number of hops, and Render's actual proxy chain can have // more hops than that — in which case req.ip resolves to an internal // Render address (e.g. 10.x.x.x) instead of the real visitor. Reading // X-Forwarded-For directly and taking the leftmost entry (the original // client, by convention — each proxy appends its own address to the // right as the request passes through) sidesteps that entirely. function getClientIp(req) { const forwarded = req.headers['x-forwarded-for']; const raw = forwarded ? forwarded.split(',')[0].trim() : (req.ip || ''); return String(raw).replace(/^::ffff:/, ''); } // Which route this request belongs to, for the per-section permission // check. Returns null for routes that aren't tied to a specific home-screen // section (e.g. the /api/portal/* self-service endpoints) — those are only // gated by login, not by module. function resolvePortalModule(req) { const p = req.path; if (p.startsWith('/api/wholesale/')) return 'wholesale'; if (p.startsWith('/api/location/')) return p.includes('/replenishment') ? 'replenishment' : 'location'; if (p.startsWith('/api/stock/') || p.startsWith('/api/stock-images/')) return 'stockinfo'; if (p.startsWith('/api/suppliers/') || p.startsWith('/api/supplier-orders/')) return 'supplier'; if (p.startsWith('/api/invoices/')) return 'invoices'; if (p.startsWith('/api/restaurant/')) return 'restaurant'; if (p.startsWith('/api/whatsapp/')) return 'whatsapp'; if (p.startsWith('/api/returns/')) return 'returns'; if (p.startsWith('/api/labels')) return 'labels'; if (p.startsWith('/api/retail/') || p.startsWith('/api/categories') || p.startsWith('/api/items') || p.startsWith('/api/goods-in/') || p.startsWith('/api/departments/margins') || p.startsWith('/api/vector-import/')) return 'picking'; return null; } // Routes that must stay completely outside the new login+IP gate: // - /health: Render's own uptime check, must always respond // - /api/portal/login: you can't require login to reach the login endpoint // - /api/staff/*, /api/management/*: separate existing login systems (HR // portal, management checklist) — untouched by this change // - the few endpoints already called by unattended scripts on shop/till // PCs (the XML order puller, the Vector import poller) rather than by // a browser — they carry no staff login token and can't be made to function isPortalFullyExempt(req) { if (req.path === '/health') return true; if (req.path === '/api/portal/login') return true; if (req.path === '/api/portal/login/verify') return true; if (req.path.startsWith('/api/staff/')) return true; if (req.path.startsWith('/api/management/')) return true; // Restaurant customer flow: customers sign in with their own passcode and // carry a separate 'restaurant' JWT, which the portal (staff) auth below // would reject as the wrong token type -> instant "Session expired". // These routes are protected by restaurantAuthMiddleware instead. The // admin routes (/api/restaurant/admin/*) are NOT exempt and stay behind // the normal staff login. if (req.path === '/api/restaurant/login') return true; if (req.path.startsWith('/api/restaurant/order/')) return true; // Product photos are used as plain (no Authorization header possible) if (req.method === 'GET' && /^\/api\/stock-images\/[^/]+$/.test(req.path)) return true; if (req.path === '/api/restaurant/orders/pending-xml') return true; if (/^\/api\/restaurant\/orders\/[^/]+\/mark-exported$/.test(req.path)) return true; if (req.path === '/api/vector-import/next') return true; if (req.method === 'POST' && /^\/api\/vector-import\/[^/]+\/status$/.test(req.path)) return true; if (req.method === 'POST' && /^\/api\/retail\/lists\/[^/]+\/track-time$/.test(req.path)) return true; // Meta calls this directly (GET to verify, POST for webhook events) — it // can't carry a portal login token, so it must bypass auth/IP/module // checks entirely, same as the other machine-to-machine endpoints above. if (req.path === '/api/whatsapp/webhook') return true; // Public PDF library links (used as WhatsApp template "Visit Website" // button URLs) — Meta's servers and customers' phones fetch these // directly with no portal login of any kind, so this must bypass // auth/IP/module checks the same way the webhook does above. Note this // ONLY matches the '/file/' serve path (GET) — the sibling // upload/list/delete routes under '/api/wholesale/flyer/pdfs' deliberately // do NOT match this and stay behind the normal staff login below. if (req.method === 'GET' && /^\/api\/wholesale\/flyer\/pdfs\/file\/[^/]+$/.test(req.path)) return true; return false; } // The portal's own self-service endpoints (see who am I, see my IP, and — // admin only — manage the allowlist/users). These require a valid login // but deliberately skip the IP-allowlist and module checks below them, // otherwise an admin logging in from a brand-new network could never // reach the "add my IP" screen to fix that. function isPortalSelfServiceRoute(req) { return req.path.startsWith('/api/portal/') && req.path !== '/api/portal/login'; } function portalAuthMiddleware(req, res, next) { const header = req.headers.authorization || ''; const token = header.startsWith('Bearer ') ? header.slice(7) : null; if (!token) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Not signed in', portalAuthRequired: true }); try { const payload = jwt.verify(token, JWT_SECRET); if (payload.type !== 'portal') throw new Error('wrong token type'); req.portalUser = payload; next(); } catch (e) { return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Session expired — please sign in again', portalAuthRequired: true }); } } // portalIpGate runs on almost every /api/* request (see app.use below), and // until now it ran a fresh PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS query — a full pool checkout — // on every single one of them, on top of whatever query the actual route // handler needed. Under real concurrent staff usage that doubles DB // connection pressure across the whole portal, which is exactly the kind // of thing that tips a busy pool into NJS-040 (connection request timeout) // even though poolMax (15) would otherwise be enough for the handlers // alone. The allowed-IP list only ever changes when an admin adds/removes // one via the endpoints below, so it's cached in memory and only re-read // from Oracle every ALLOWED_IPS_CACHE_MS, or immediately after an admin // change (see invalidateAllowedIpsCache calls below) — same access rules, // far fewer round trips. const ALLOWED_IPS_CACHE_MS = 30 * 1000; let allowedIpsCache = { set: null, at: 0 }; function invalidateAllowedIpsCache() { allowedIpsCache = { set: null, at: 0 }; } async function getAllowedIpsSet() { const now = Date.now(); if (allowedIpsCache.set && (now - allowedIpsCache.at) < ALLOWED_IPS_CACHE_MS) { return allowedIpsCache.set; } const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute(`SELECT IP_ADDRESS FROM PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS`)); const set = new Set(lower(result.rows).map(r => r.ip_address)); allowedIpsCache = { set, at: now }; return set; } async function isIpAllowed(ip) { const set = await getAllowedIpsSet(); return set.has(ip); } async function portalIpGate(req, res, next) { try { const ip = getClientIp(req); const allowed = await isIpAllowed(ip); if (allowed) return next(); if (req.portalUser && req.portalUser.role === 'admin') { // Admins get told their IP so the frontend can offer "add this // network" instead of just a dead end. return res.status(403).json({ error: 'This network is not on the allowed list yet.', ipNotAllowed: true, yourIp: ip }); } return res.status(403).json({ error: 'This portal cannot be used from this network. Ask an admin to add it.', ipNotAllowed: true }); } catch (e) { console.error('portalIpGate error', e); return res.status(500).json({ error: 'Could not verify network access' }); } } function portalModuleGate(req, res, next) { const mod = resolvePortalModule(req); if (!mod) return next(); // not tied to a specific section if (req.portalUser.role === 'admin') return next(); const modules = req.portalUser.modules || []; if (modules.includes(mod)) return next(); return res.status(403).json({ error: 'You do not have access to this section.' }); } function requirePortalAdmin(req, res, next) { if (!req.portalUser || req.portalUser.role !== 'admin') { return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Admin access required' }); } next(); } // Applied to every /api/* request, in order: logged in? -> on an allowed // network (unless self-service or admin fixing that right now)? -> allowed // to see this section (unless self-service)? app.use((req, res, next) => { if (!req.path.startsWith('/api/') || isPortalFullyExempt(req)) return next(); return portalAuthMiddleware(req, res, next); }); app.use((req, res, next) => { if (!req.path.startsWith('/api/') || isPortalFullyExempt(req) || isPortalSelfServiceRoute(req)) return next(); return portalIpGate(req, res, next); }); app.use((req, res, next) => { if (!req.path.startsWith('/api/') || isPortalFullyExempt(req) || isPortalSelfServiceRoute(req)) return next(); return portalModuleGate(req, res, next); }); // ---- Login, step 1: { name, passcode } -> emails a 6-digit code. // Two-factor: knowing the passcode alone is no longer enough to get a // token — every login (not just new devices) also requires the code // just sent to that user's registered email. ---- const OTP_TTL_MINUTES = 10; const OTP_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 5; function generateOtpCode() { // 6 digits, zero-padded (e.g. "042613"), via crypto for unpredictability. return String(crypto.randomInt(0, 1000000)).padStart(6, '0'); } app.post('/api/portal/login', async (req, res) => { const { name, passcode } = req.body || {}; const enteredName = String(name || '').trim(); const enteredPasscode = String(passcode || '').trim(); if (!enteredName || !enteredPasscode) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Enter your username and passcode' }); try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT USER_ID, NAME, EMAIL, PASSCODE_HASH, ROLE, MODULES FROM PORTAL_USERS WHERE ACTIVE = 1 AND UPPER(NAME) = UPPER(:name)`, { name: enteredName } )); const users = lower(result.rows); // Username is looked up first (not scanned like the old passcode-only // flow), then the passcode is checked only against that one user — // matches how a normal username+password login behaves. const matched = users[0]; const passcodeOk = matched ? await bcrypt.compare(enteredPasscode, matched.passcode_hash) : false; if (!matched || !passcodeOk) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Username or passcode not recognised' }); if (!matched.email) return res.status(500).json({ error: 'No email on file for this account — ask an admin to add one before you can sign in' }); const code = generateOtpCode(); const codeHash = await bcrypt.hash(code, 10); // BUG FIX: this DELETE was missing { autoCommit: true }. withConnection() // closes the connection right after the query runs, and a pooled // connection release implicitly rolls back any uncommitted transaction — // so this delete of the user's old OTP row was never actually // persisted. Old rows piled up in PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP on every login // attempt instead of being cleared, and the verify step below could // then match against a stale row instead of the one just emailed. // That's what was producing "Incorrect code" even for a correct code. await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `DELETE FROM PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP WHERE USER_ID = :userId`, { userId: matched.user_id }, { autoCommit: true } )); await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `INSERT INTO PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP (USER_ID, CODE_HASH, EXPIRES_AT) VALUES (:userId, :codeHash, CURRENT_TIMESTAMP + INTERVAL '${OTP_TTL_MINUTES}' MINUTE)`, { userId: matched.user_id, codeHash }, { autoCommit: true } )); try { await sendMail({ to: matched.email, subject: 'Hallan Portal — your sign-in code', text: `Your sign-in code is ${code}. It expires in ${OTP_TTL_MINUTES} minutes. If this wasn't you, ignore this email.` }); } catch (mailErr) { console.error('portal OTP email failed', mailErr); return res.status(500).json({ error: "Couldn't send the code email — check BREVO_API_KEY/MAIL_FROM, or try again" }); } // Deliberately no token yet, and the name isn't echoed back beyond // what the user already typed — step 2 needs userId, which is safe // to hand back since it's meaningless without the emailed code too. res.json({ requiresOtp: true, userId: matched.user_id }); } catch (e) { console.error('portal login error', e); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Login failed, please try again' }); } }); // ---- Login, step 2: { userId, code } -> JWT. ---- app.post('/api/portal/login/verify', async (req, res) => { const { userId, code } = req.body || {}; const enteredCode = String(code || '').trim(); if (!userId || !enteredCode) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Enter the code from your email' }); try { // Defensive ordering: even after the autoCommit fix above, any rows left // over from before this fix shipped (or a rare concurrent-login race) // should never win over the code that was actually just emailed — so // always take the newest row (highest OTP_ID) and discard the rest. const otpResult = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT OTP_ID, CODE_HASH, EXPIRES_AT, ATTEMPTS FROM PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP WHERE USER_ID = :userId ORDER BY OTP_ID DESC`, { userId } )); const otpRows = lower(otpResult.rows); const otp = otpRows[0]; if (!otp) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'No code pending — please sign in again' }); if (otpRows.length > 1) { // Clean up stale duplicates so they can't interfere with a future login. await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `DELETE FROM PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP WHERE USER_ID = :userId AND OTP_ID != :keepId`, { userId, keepId: otp.otp_id }, { autoCommit: true } )); } if (new Date(otp.expires_at).getTime() < Date.now()) { await withConnection(conn => conn.execute(`DELETE FROM PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP WHERE OTP_ID = :id`, { id: otp.otp_id }, { autoCommit: true })); return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Code expired — please sign in again' }); } if (otp.attempts >= OTP_MAX_ATTEMPTS) { await withConnection(conn => conn.execute(`DELETE FROM PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP WHERE OTP_ID = :id`, { id: otp.otp_id }, { autoCommit: true })); return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Too many attempts — please sign in again' }); } const codeOk = await bcrypt.compare(enteredCode, otp.code_hash); if (!codeOk) { await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `UPDATE PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP SET ATTEMPTS = ATTEMPTS + 1 WHERE OTP_ID = :id`, { id: otp.otp_id }, { autoCommit: true } )); return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Incorrect code' }); } const userResult = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT USER_ID, NAME, ROLE, MODULES FROM PORTAL_USERS WHERE USER_ID = :userId AND ACTIVE = 1`, { userId } )); const matched = lower(userResult.rows)[0]; if (!matched) return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Account no longer active' }); await withConnection(conn => conn.execute(`DELETE FROM PORTAL_LOGIN_OTP WHERE OTP_ID = :id`, { id: otp.otp_id }, { autoCommit: true })); const modules = (matched.modules || '').split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean); const token = jwt.sign( { type: 'portal', userId: matched.user_id, name: matched.name, role: matched.role, modules }, JWT_SECRET, { expiresIn: PORTAL_TOKEN_TTL } ); const yourIp = getClientIp(req); const ipAllowed = await isIpAllowed(yourIp); res.json({ token, name: matched.name, role: matched.role, modules, ipAllowed, yourIp }); } catch (e) { console.error('portal login verify error', e); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Verification failed, please try again' }); } }); app.get('/api/portal/me', (req, res) => { res.json({ name: req.portalUser.name, role: req.portalUser.role, modules: req.portalUser.modules || [] }); }); // Lets the frontend show "your current IP is X" without relying on an // external "what's my IP" site — this is the actual IP the server sees, // which is the one that matters for the allowlist. app.get('/api/portal/my-ip', (req, res) => { res.json({ ip: getClientIp(req) }); }); app.get('/api/portal/modules', requirePortalAdmin, (req, res) => { res.json({ modules: PORTAL_MODULES }); }); // ---- Allowed IPs: admin only ---- app.get('/api/portal/allowed-ips', requirePortalAdmin, async (req, res) => { try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT IP_ID, IP_ADDRESS, LABEL, ADDED_BY, CREATED_AT FROM PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS ORDER BY CREATED_AT DESC` )); res.json({ items: lower(result.rows) }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.post('/api/portal/allowed-ips', requirePortalAdmin, async (req, res) => { const ip = String((req.body || {}).ip_address || '').trim(); const label = String((req.body || {}).label || '').trim() || null; if (!ip) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'ip_address is required' }); try { const existing = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT 1 FROM PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS WHERE IP_ADDRESS = :ip`, { ip } )); if (existing.rows.length) return res.status(409).json({ error: 'That IP is already on the list' }); await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `INSERT INTO PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS (IP_ADDRESS, LABEL, ADDED_BY) VALUES (:ip, :label, :addedBy)`, { ip, label, addedBy: req.portalUser.name }, { autoCommit: true } )); invalidateAllowedIpsCache(); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.delete('/api/portal/allowed-ips/:id', requirePortalAdmin, async (req, res) => { try { await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `DELETE FROM PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS WHERE IP_ID = :id`, { id: req.params.id }, { autoCommit: true } )); invalidateAllowedIpsCache(); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); // ---- Users: admin only ---- app.get('/api/portal/users', requirePortalAdmin, async (req, res) => { try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT USER_ID, NAME, EMAIL, ROLE, MODULES, ACTIVE, CREATED_AT FROM PORTAL_USERS ORDER BY NAME` )); res.json({ items: lower(result.rows).map(u => ({ ...u, modules: (u.modules || '').split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean) })) }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.post('/api/portal/users', requirePortalAdmin, async (req, res) => { const { name, email, passcode, role, modules } = req.body || {}; const cleanName = String(name || '').trim(); const cleanEmail = String(email || '').trim(); const cleanPasscode = String(passcode || '').trim(); if (!cleanName || !cleanEmail || !cleanPasscode) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'name, email and passcode are required' }); if (!isValidEmail(cleanEmail)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Enter a valid email address' }); if (cleanPasscode.length < 4) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Passcode should be at least 4 characters' }); const cleanRole = role === 'admin' ? 'admin' : 'staff'; const modulesStr = Array.isArray(modules) ? modules.filter(Boolean).join(',') : ''; try { const hash = await bcrypt.hash(cleanPasscode, 10); await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `INSERT INTO PORTAL_USERS (NAME, EMAIL, PASSCODE_HASH, ROLE, MODULES, ACTIVE) VALUES (:name, :email, :hash, :role, :modules, 1)`, { name: cleanName, email: cleanEmail, hash, role: cleanRole, modules: modulesStr }, { autoCommit: true } )); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.patch('/api/portal/users/:id', requirePortalAdmin, async (req, res) => { const { name, email, passcode, role, modules, active } = req.body || {}; const sets = []; const binds = { id: req.params.id }; if (name && String(name).trim()) { sets.push('NAME = :name'); binds.name = String(name).trim(); } if (email !== undefined) { const cleanEmail = String(email || '').trim(); if (!isValidEmail(cleanEmail)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Enter a valid email address' }); sets.push('EMAIL = :email'); binds.email = cleanEmail; } if (role) { sets.push('ROLE = :role'); binds.role = role === 'admin' ? 'admin' : 'staff'; } if (Array.isArray(modules)) { sets.push('MODULES = :modules'); binds.modules = modules.filter(Boolean).join(','); } if (typeof active === 'boolean') { sets.push('ACTIVE = :active'); binds.active = active ? 1 : 0; } if (passcode && String(passcode).trim()) { if (String(passcode).trim().length < 4) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Passcode should be at least 4 characters' }); binds.hash = await bcrypt.hash(String(passcode).trim(), 10); sets.push('PASSCODE_HASH = :hash'); } if (sets.length === 0) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Nothing to update' }); try { await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `UPDATE PORTAL_USERS SET ${sets.join(', ')} WHERE USER_ID = :id`, binds, { autoCommit: true } )); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.delete('/api/portal/users/:id', requirePortalAdmin, async (req, res) => { if (String(req.params.id) === String(req.portalUser.userId)) { return res.status(400).json({ error: "You can't delete your own account while signed in as it" }); } try { await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `DELETE FROM PORTAL_USERS WHERE USER_ID = :id`, { id: req.params.id }, { autoCommit: true } )); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.get('/health', (req, res) => res.json({ status: 'ok' })); // ---- Stock search (autocomplete) — lenient: matches words in any order, tolerates small typos ---- app.get('/api/stock/search', async (req, res) => { const q = (req.query.q || '').trim(); if (!q) return res.json({ items: [] }); try { // Pull a broad candidate set, ranked by how many query words each row matches, // so common words (like "10kg" or "atta") don't crowd out the actual target // before our precise fuzzy-matching step gets to see it. const words = q.toLowerCase().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean); const binds = {}; const matchExprs = words.map((w, i) => { binds['w' + i] = w; return `CASE WHEN UPPER(NAME) LIKE '%' || UPPER(:w${i}) || '%' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END`; }); const orClauses = words.map((w, i) => `UPPER(NAME) LIKE '%' || UPPER(:w${i}) || '%'`).join(' OR '); const matchCountExpr = matchExprs.join(' + '); const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT STOCKID, NAME, QUANTITY, REORDERLEVEL, MAXSTOCKLEVEL, DEPTID FROM ( SELECT STOCKID, NAME, QUANTITY, REORDERLEVEL, MAXSTOCKLEVEL, DEPTID, (${matchCountExpr}) AS MATCH_COUNT FROM VESTOCK WHERE ${orClauses} ) ORDER BY MATCH_COUNT DESC FETCH FIRST 800 ROWS ONLY`, binds )); const candidates = lower(result.rows); const scored = candidates .map(row => ({ row, score: scoreMatch(q, row.name) })) .filter(x => x.score !== null) .sort((a, b) => b.score.matchedCount - a.score.matchedCount || a.score.distance - b.score.distance) .slice(0, 40) .map(x => x.row); res.json({ items: scored }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); // ---- Single item live stock ---- app.get('/api/stock/item', async (req, res) => { const name = req.query.name || ''; try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT NAME, QUANTITY, REORDERLEVEL, MAXSTOCKLEVEL, DEPTID FROM VESTOCK WHERE NAME = :name`, { name } )); res.json({ items: lower(result.rows) }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); // ---- Current retail price for one stockid (PRICEID = 1 = Retail) — used // when staff re-points a Create Invoice line at a different product, // so the Retail Price box updates to the newly-matched product's price // instead of keeping the old match's price. ---- app.get('/api/stock/:stockid/retail-price', async (req, res) => { try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT PRICE_NET FROM STOCK_PRICES WHERE STOCKID = :stockid AND PRICEID = 1`, { stockid: req.params.stockid } )); const rows = lower(result.rows); res.json({ retail_price: rows[0] ? rows[0].price_net : null }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); // ---- Barcode lookup: barcode -> stock item ---- // Requires a VEBARCODE table (BARCODE, STOCKID) synced from Firebird. See test_oracle.py. app.get('/api/stock/barcode/:code', async (req, res) => { const code = (req.params.code || '').trim(); if (!code) return res.json({ item: null }); try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `SELECT s.STOCKID, s.NAME, s.QUANTITY, s.REORDERLEVEL, s.MAXSTOCKLEVEL, s.DEPTID, b.BARCODE FROM VEBARCODES b JOIN VESTOCK s ON s.STOCKID = b.STOCKID WHERE b.BARCODE = :code`, { code } )); const rows = lower(result.rows); res.json({ item: rows[0] || null }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); /* --------------------------------------------------------- STOCK INFO: one search box, barcode or name, returns Stock Name / Barcode / Location / In Stock in one shot. "Location" is pulled from LOCATION_ROW_ITEMS (see the Store Locations section below) if the item has been filed in a zone/shelf/row; otherwise it comes back blank. --------------------------------------------------------- */ app.get('/api/stock/info', async (req, res) => { const q = (req.query.q || '').trim(); if (!q) return res.json({ items: [] }); try { const items = await withConnection(async conn => { // 1) Exact barcode match, if any — always ranked first const barcodeResult = await conn.execute( `SELECT s.STOCKID FROM VEBARCODES b JOIN VESTOCK s ON s.STOCKID = b.STOCKID WHERE b.BARCODE = :q`, { q } ); const barcodeMatchIds = lower(barcodeResult.rows).map(r => r.stockid); // 2) Fuzzy multi-word name match, same ranking approach as /api/stock/search const words = q.toLowerCase().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean); const binds = {}; const matchExprs = words.map((w, i) => { binds['w' + i] = w; return `CASE WHEN UPPER(NAME) LIKE '%' || UPPER(:w${i}) || '%' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END`; }); const orClauses = words.map((w, i) => `UPPER(NAME) LIKE '%' || UPPER(:w${i}) || '%'`).join(' OR '); const nameResult = orClauses ? await conn.execute( `SELECT STOCKID FROM ( SELECT STOCKID, (${matchExprs.join(' + ')}) AS MATCH_COUNT FROM VESTOCK WHERE ${orClauses} ) ORDER BY MATCH_COUNT DESC FETCH FIRST 40 ROWS ONLY`, binds ) : { rows: [] }; // Merge: barcode match(es) first, then name matches, de-duplicated, order preserved const seen = new Set(); const orderedIds = []; barcodeMatchIds.forEach(id => { if (!seen.has(id)) { seen.add(id); orderedIds.push(id); } }); lower(nameResult.rows).forEach(r => { if (!seen.has(r.stockid)) { seen.add(r.stockid); orderedIds.push(r.stockid); } }); if (orderedIds.length === 0) return []; // 3) Pull full detail for the matched stockids: name, quantity, all barcodes, location. // These used to be LEFT JOINs against subqueries that GROUP BY the *entire* // VEBARCODES / LOCATION_ROW_ITEMS tables before filtering — meaning every // search re-aggregated every barcode and every filed location in the whole // database, no matter how few stockids matched. Correlated subqueries scoped // to `= s.STOCKID` do the same aggregation but only for the handful of rows // that matched, so this scales with the search result, not the table size. const idBinds = {}; const idPlaceholders = orderedIds.map((id, i) => { idBinds['id' + i] = id; return ':id' + i; }).join(','); const detailResult = await conn.execute( `SELECT s.STOCKID, s.NAME, s.QUANTITY, (SELECT LISTAGG(b.BARCODE, ', ') WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY b.BARCODE) FROM VEBARCODES b WHERE b.STOCKID = s.STOCKID) AS BARCODES, COALESCE( (SELECT LISTAGG(z.ZONE_NAME || ' / ' || sh.SHELF_NAME || ' / Row ' || a.ROW_NAME, '; ') WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY z.ZONE_NAME, sh.SHELF_NAME, a.ROW_NAME) FROM LOCATION_ROW_ITEMS li JOIN LOCATION_ROWS a ON a.ROW_ID = li.ROW_ID JOIN LOCATION_SHELVES sh ON sh.SHELF_ID = a.SHELF_ID JOIN LOCATION_ZONES z ON z.ZONE_ID = sh.ZONE_ID WHERE li.STOCKID = s.STOCKID), (SELECT LISTAGG(z.ZONE_NAME || ' / ' || sh.SHELF_NAME || ' / Row ' || a.ROW_NAME, '; ') WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY z.ZONE_NAME, sh.SHELF_NAME, a.ROW_NAME) FROM LOCATION_ROW_ITEMS li JOIN LOCATION_ROWS a ON a.ROW_ID = li.ROW_ID JOIN LOCATION_SHELVES sh ON sh.SHELF_ID = a.SHELF_ID JOIN LOCATION_ZONES z ON z.ZONE_ID = sh.ZONE_ID WHERE li.STOCKID IS NULL AND li.ITEM_NAME = s.NAME) ) AS LOCATIONS FROM VESTOCK s WHERE s.STOCKID IN (${idPlaceholders})`, idBinds ); const byId = new Map(lower(detailResult.rows).map(r => [r.stockid, r])); return orderedIds.map(id => byId.get(id)).filter(Boolean); }); res.json({ items }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); /* --------------------------------------------------------- WHOLESALE PRICING LIST (read-only reference list) Populated by test_oracle.py from the Wholesale/Trade 3 CTE query against Firebird — this endpoint just reads the synced Oracle table. Requires the WHOLESALE_PRICING table — see the CREATE TABLE statement noted alongside this file. PERFORMANCE (load-time fix): the query below used to run three correlated subqueries (barcode, pack size, supplier name) PER ROW of WHOLESALE_PRICING, each one independently re-scanning VESTOCK / SUPPLIER_STOCK / SUPPLIERS from scratch with a non-sargable UPPER(TRIM(...)) comparison. For a few hundred wholesale rows against a multi-thousand-row VESTOCK table, that's effectively an N×M nested loop three times over — the actual cause of the slow load, not network/frontend rendering. Rewritten below to compute the name-normalisation ONCE per VESTOCK row (name_norm CTE), aggregate barcodes/suppliers ONCE with GROUP BY / ROW_NUMBER(), and then LEFT JOIN those pre-computed sets onto WHOLESALE_PRICING a single time. Same matching rules, same tie-breaks, same result set — just computed as set-based joins instead of row-by-row subqueries, which lets Oracle use a hash/merge join and a couple of full scans instead of thousands of tiny lookups. If DBA access is available, these two indexes make it faster still (optional, not required): CREATE INDEX IDX_VESTOCK_NAME_NORM ON VESTOCK (UPPER(TRIM(NAME))); CREATE INDEX IDX_SUPPLIER_STOCK_STOCKID ON SUPPLIER_STOCK (STOCKID); Also cached in-memory for WHOLESALE_PRICING_CACHE_MS (see below) since the underlying table only changes when test_oracle.py runs its sync — not on every page open — so repeat opens of the list by different staff don't each re-run the full query. Pass ?refresh=1 to bypass the cache and force a fresh read straight after a sync. --------------------------------------------------------- */ const WHOLESALE_PRICING_SQL = ` WITH name_norm AS ( SELECT STOCKID, UPPER(TRIM(NAME)) AS NKEY, QUANTITY FROM VESTOCK ), barcode_pick AS ( SELECT nn.NKEY, MIN(b.BARCODE) AS BARCODE FROM name_norm nn JOIN VEBARCODES b ON b.STOCKID = nn.STOCKID GROUP BY nn.NKEY ), -- Feature 4 (Stock button): live on-hand quantity, matched the same way -- as everything else on this page — by normalised STOCK_NAME, not -- STOCKID (WHOLESALE_PRICING has no STOCKID of its own). SUM covers the -- rare case of more than one VESTOCK row sharing a normalised name, same -- as barcode_pick above aggregates per NKEY instead of assuming 1:1. stock_pick AS ( SELECT NKEY, SUM(QUANTITY) AS QUANTITY FROM name_norm GROUP BY NKEY ), supplier_ranked AS ( SELECT wp.STOCK_NAME AS SNAME, ss.PACKSIZE, sup.NAME AS SUPPLIER_NAME, ROW_NUMBER() OVER ( PARTITION BY wp.STOCK_NAME ORDER BY ABS(NVL(ss.COST_NET, 0) - NVL(wp.SUPPCOST_NET, 0)), ss.SUPPID ) AS RN FROM WHOLESALE_PRICING wp JOIN name_norm nn ON nn.NKEY = UPPER(TRIM(wp.STOCK_NAME)) JOIN SUPPLIER_STOCK ss ON ss.STOCKID = nn.STOCKID JOIN SUPPLIERS sup ON sup.SUPPID = ss.SUPPID ) SELECT wp.STOCK_NAME, wp.SUPPCOST_NET, CASE WHEN wp.VATID = 1 AND wp.PRICE_VAT = 0 THEN wp.PRICE_NET / 1.2 ELSE wp.PRICE_NET END AS PRICE_NET, wp.PRICENAME, wp.DEPARTMENT_NAME, wp.VATID, bp.BARCODE, sr.PACKSIZE AS PACK_SIZE, -- Kit items are never stocked directly, so their VESTOCK.QUANTITY -- (and therefore sp.QUANTITY here) always comes back 0/NULL even -- when the kit's components are in stock. wp.KIT_STOCK_QTY is the -- buildable-kit count computed from those components on the -- Firebird side (test_oracle.py's KITSTOCK CTE) and is NULL for -- non-kit items, so this only ever substitutes for a kit, never -- masks a genuinely out-of-stock non-kit item. CASE WHEN NVL(sp.QUANTITY, 0) = 0 AND wp.KIT_STOCK_QTY IS NOT NULL THEN wp.KIT_STOCK_QTY ELSE sp.QUANTITY END AS STOCK_QUANTITY, -- Kit items have no SUPPLIER_STOCK row of their own (see the -- KITSUPPLIER CTE comment in test_oracle.py), so supplier_ranked -- above misses for them. KIT_SUPPLIER_NAME is resolved on the -- Firebird side from the kit's actual components — this COALESCE -- is the only place that value gets used. COALESCE(sr.SUPPLIER_NAME, wp.KIT_SUPPLIER_NAME) AS SUPPLIER_NAME FROM WHOLESALE_PRICING wp LEFT JOIN barcode_pick bp ON bp.NKEY = UPPER(TRIM(wp.STOCK_NAME)) LEFT JOIN stock_pick sp ON sp.NKEY = UPPER(TRIM(wp.STOCK_NAME)) LEFT JOIN supplier_ranked sr ON sr.SNAME = wp.STOCK_NAME AND sr.RN = 1 ORDER BY wp.DEPARTMENT_NAME, wp.STOCK_NAME`; const WHOLESALE_PRICING_CACHE_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000; // 3 minutes — plenty fresh vs. the sync's own schedule let wholesalePricingCache = { rows: null, at: 0 }; async function fetchWholesalePricingRows(conn) { const result = await conn.execute(WHOLESALE_PRICING_SQL); return lower(result.rows); } // Shared by the GET endpoint below and the scheduled CSV runner, so both // paths always see the exact same cost/price data. async function getWholesalePricingRows(forceRefresh) { const now = Date.now(); if (!forceRefresh && wholesalePricingCache.rows && (now - wholesalePricingCache.at) < WHOLESALE_PRICING_CACHE_MS) { return wholesalePricingCache.rows; } const rows = await withConnection(conn => fetchWholesalePricingRows(conn)); wholesalePricingCache = { rows, at: now }; return rows; } app.get('/api/wholesale/pricing', async (req, res) => { try { const forceRefresh = req.query.refresh === '1'; const rows = await getWholesalePricingRows(forceRefresh); res.json({ items: rows }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); /* --------------------------------------------------------- WHOLESALE SELLING PRICE LOCK ("Block" / "Release") Lets staff lock in the selling prices they've manually typed on the Wholesale Pricing List so a fresh Firebird sync (new cost/list price from test_oracle.py) doesn't silently overwrite them on next page load. Supplier Cost always reflects the latest sync regardless — only the Selling Price / Margin the frontend seeds into each row is affected by this. - "Block": snapshots whatever is currently on screen into WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK and flips WHOLESALE_PRICES_BLOCKED on in APP_SETTINGS. While blocked, every further edit a person makes to a row's price/margin is also saved here immediately (see /price-lock/update), so it survives the next refresh too. - "Release": clears every locked row and flips the flag back off, so the list goes back to always showing the freshly-synced default price on load (today's behaviour). Requires: CREATE TABLE WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK ( STOCK_NAME VARCHAR2(500) PRIMARY KEY, LOCKED_PRICE NUMBER, LOCKED_MARGIN NUMBER, CHANGED_FLAG NUMBER(1) DEFAULT 0 NOT NULL, LOCKED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT SYSTIMESTAMP ); If this table already exists from before CHANGED_FLAG was added, run: ALTER TABLE WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK ADD (CHANGED_FLAG NUMBER(1) DEFAULT 0 NOT NULL); CHANGED_FLAG distinguishes "this row was actually edited after Block" from "this row simply has a snapshot because Block saves every row that was on screen at the time" — without it, every locked row looked identical to a genuinely-edited one on the next reload, which made the on-screen "price changed" indicator meaningless. It's only ever set to 1 from /price-lock/update (fired by a real price/margin edit while blocked) or when the initial Block snapshot already carried a change made earlier in the same session; it's never implied purely by a row having a lock record. Release wipes it along with everything else in this table. Reuses the existing APP_SETTINGS table (see the Wholesale Pricing Password section above) for the WHOLESALE_PRICES_BLOCKED flag — no extra table needed for that part. --------------------------------------------------------- */ async function getWholesaleBlockedFlag(conn) { const v = await getStoredSetting(conn, 'WHOLESALE_PRICES_BLOCKED'); return v === '1'; } async function setWholesaleBlockedFlag(conn, blocked) { await conn.execute( `MERGE INTO APP_SETTINGS s USING (SELECT 'WHOLESALE_PRICES_BLOCKED' AS k, :v AS v FROM dual) d ON (s.SETTING_KEY = d.k) WHEN MATCHED THEN UPDATE SET s.SETTING_VALUE = d.v WHEN NOT MATCHED THEN INSERT (SETTING_KEY, SETTING_VALUE) VALUES (d.k, d.v)`, { v: blocked ? '1' : '0' } ); } async function getWholesalePriceLocks(conn) { const result = await conn.execute(`SELECT STOCK_NAME, LOCKED_PRICE, LOCKED_MARGIN, CHANGED_FLAG FROM WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK`); const map = {}; for (const r of lower(result.rows)) { map[r.stock_name] = { price: r.locked_price, margin: r.locked_margin, changed: r.changed_flag === 1 }; } return map; } async function upsertWholesalePriceLock(conn, stockName, price, margin, changed) { await conn.execute( `MERGE INTO WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK t USING (SELECT :name AS n FROM dual) d ON (t.STOCK_NAME = d.n) WHEN MATCHED THEN UPDATE SET t.LOCKED_PRICE = :price, t.LOCKED_MARGIN = :margin, t.CHANGED_FLAG = :changed, t.LOCKED_AT = SYSTIMESTAMP WHEN NOT MATCHED THEN INSERT (STOCK_NAME, LOCKED_PRICE, LOCKED_MARGIN, CHANGED_FLAG) VALUES (:name, :price, :margin, :changed)`, { name: stockName, price: price === null || price === undefined || price === '' ? null : Number(price), margin: margin === null || margin === undefined || margin === '' ? null : Number(margin), changed: changed ? 1 : 0 } ); } // The "Block" button snapshots EVERY row currently on screen — for the full // Wholesale list that can be hundreds/thousands of rows. The original code // looped upsertWholesalePriceLock() one row at a time, i.e. one network // round-trip to Oracle per row — that serial round-tripping, not the // browser or the query itself, was the actual cause of "Block" taking a // long time with nothing visible happening. Rewritten to use oracledb's // executeMany(), which sends the whole batch as ONE round-trip instead of // N, the same fix in spirit as the earlier N+1 query rewrite for load speed. async function upsertWholesalePriceLocksBulk(conn, prices) { const clean = prices.filter(p => p && p.stock_name); if (clean.length === 0) return; const binds = clean.map(p => ({ name: p.stock_name, price: p.price === null || p.price === undefined || p.price === '' ? null : Number(p.price), margin: p.margin === null || p.margin === undefined || p.margin === '' ? null : Number(p.margin), // Carries over whatever the browser already knew about this row at the // moment Block was clicked (true only if it was edited earlier in the // same session) — Block itself never manufactures a "changed" row. changed: p.changed ? 1 : 0 })); await conn.executeMany( `MERGE INTO WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK t USING (SELECT :name AS n FROM dual) d ON (t.STOCK_NAME = d.n) WHEN MATCHED THEN UPDATE SET t.LOCKED_PRICE = :price, t.LOCKED_MARGIN = :margin, t.CHANGED_FLAG = :changed, t.LOCKED_AT = SYSTIMESTAMP WHEN NOT MATCHED THEN INSERT (STOCK_NAME, LOCKED_PRICE, LOCKED_MARGIN, CHANGED_FLAG) VALUES (:name, :price, :margin, :changed)`, binds ); } // ---- Current lock state: whether blocked, and every locked price/margin. ---- app.get('/api/wholesale/price-lock', async (req, res) => { try { const data = await withConnection(async conn => { const blocked = await getWholesaleBlockedFlag(conn); const prices = await getWholesalePriceLocks(conn); return { blocked, prices }; }); res.json(data); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); // ---- "Block" button: snapshot whatever's currently on screen and turn locking on. ---- app.post('/api/wholesale/price-lock/block', async (req, res) => { const { prices } = req.body || {}; try { await withConnection(async conn => { if (Array.isArray(prices) && prices.length) { await upsertWholesalePriceLocksBulk(conn, prices); } await setWholesaleBlockedFlag(conn, true); await conn.commit(); }); res.json({ ok: true, blocked: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); // ---- Saves one row's edit while blocked, so it survives the next refresh // without waiting for the person to click Block again. ---- app.post('/api/wholesale/price-lock/update', async (req, res) => { const { stock_name, price, margin } = req.body || {}; if (!stock_name) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'stock_name is required' }); try { await withConnection(async conn => { // This endpoint only ever fires from an actual price/margin edit while // blocked (see persistWholesaleLockIfBlocked in the frontend), so it's // always a genuine change — unlike the initial Block snapshot, which // isn't. await upsertWholesalePriceLock(conn, stock_name, price, margin, true); await conn.commit(); }); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); // ---- "Release" button: clear every lock and turn locking off. ---- app.post('/api/wholesale/price-lock/release', async (req, res) => { try { await withConnection(async conn => { await conn.execute(`DELETE FROM WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK`); await setWholesaleBlockedFlag(conn, false); await conn.commit(); }); res.json({ ok: true, blocked: false }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); /* --------------------------------------------------------- WHOLESALE PRICE CHANGES SHEET Backs the "Price Changes Sheet" button on the Wholesale Pricing List. Logs every price/margin edit the moment it happens, regardless of Block/Release above (WHOLESALE_PRICE_LOCK only ever saves while Blocked) — always against ORIGINAL_PRICE/ORIGINAL_MARGIN, the value actually synced from Vector the first time a given item was edited — so the sheet keeps reading "was £X, now £Y" for whoever updates Vector by hand, however many times a price gets tweaked in between. One row per item (STOCK_NAME is the primary key): a repeat edit just updates NEW_PRICE/NEW_MARGIN/LAST_CHANGED_AT via the MERGE below, rather than piling up duplicate history rows. Cleared out entirely once someone confirms the new prices are actually live in Vector (see /price-changes/clear) — this is a working sheet, not a permanent log. Requires: CREATE TABLE WHOLESALE_PRICE_CHANGE_LOG ( STOCK_NAME VARCHAR2(500) PRIMARY KEY, BARCODE VARCHAR2(100), DEPARTMENT_NAME VARCHAR2(300), ORIGINAL_PRICE NUMBER, ORIGINAL_MARGIN NUMBER, NEW_PRICE NUMBER, NEW_MARGIN NUMBER, IS_VAT NUMBER(1) DEFAULT 0, FIRST_CHANGED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT SYSTIMESTAMP, LAST_CHANGED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT SYSTIMESTAMP ); --------------------------------------------------------- */ app.get('/api/wholesale/price-changes', async (req, res) => { try { const items = await withConnection(async conn => { const result = await conn.execute( `SELECT STOCK_NAME, BARCODE, DEPARTMENT_NAME, ORIGINAL_PRICE, ORIGINAL_MARGIN, NEW_PRICE, NEW_MARGIN, IS_VAT, FIRST_CHANGED_AT, LAST_CHANGED_AT FROM WHOLESALE_PRICE_CHANGE_LOG ORDER BY DEPARTMENT_NAME, STOCK_NAME` ); return lower(result.rows).map(r => ({ stock_name: r.stock_name, barcode: r.barcode, department: r.department_name, original_price: r.original_price, original_margin: r.original_margin, new_price: r.new_price, new_margin: r.new_margin, is_vat: r.is_vat === 1 })); }); res.json({ items }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.post('/api/wholesale/price-changes/log', async (req, res) => { const { stock_name, barcode, department, original_price, original_margin, new_price, new_margin, is_vat } = req.body || {}; if (!stock_name) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'stock_name is required' }); try { await withConnection(async conn => { await conn.execute( `MERGE INTO WHOLESALE_PRICE_CHANGE_LOG t USING (SELECT :name AS n FROM dual) d ON (t.STOCK_NAME = d.n) WHEN MATCHED THEN UPDATE SET t.NEW_PRICE = :new_price, t.NEW_MARGIN = :new_margin, t.LAST_CHANGED_AT = SYSTIMESTAMP WHEN NOT MATCHED THEN INSERT (STOCK_NAME, BARCODE, DEPARTMENT_NAME, ORIGINAL_PRICE, ORIGINAL_MARGIN, NEW_PRICE, NEW_MARGIN, IS_VAT) VALUES (:name, :barcode, :department, :original_price, :original_margin, :new_price, :new_margin, :is_vat)`, { name: stock_name, barcode: barcode || null, department: department || null, original_price: (original_price === null || original_price === undefined) ? null : Number(original_price), original_margin: (original_margin === null || original_margin === undefined) ? null : Number(original_margin), new_price: (new_price === null || new_price === undefined) ? null : Number(new_price), new_margin: (new_margin === null || new_margin === undefined) ? null : Number(new_margin), is_vat: is_vat ? 1 : 0 }, { autoCommit: true } ); }); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.post('/api/wholesale/price-changes/clear', async (req, res) => { try { await withConnection(conn => conn.execute(`DELETE FROM WHOLESALE_PRICE_CHANGE_LOG`, {}, { autoCommit: true })); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); /* --------------------------------------------------------- WHOLESALE FLYER — PUBLISH AS A GITHUB PAGES WEBSITE Backs "Update Wholesale Flyer Webpage" / "Update Veg Flyer Website" on the Wholesale Pricing List. The browser already builds the full, self-contained flyer HTML (same images/prices as Download Colour Flyer — see buildFlyerWebpageHTML in index.html) and POSTs it here; this endpoint just commits it straight to a GitHub Pages repo. Deliberately NOT served from this portal: portal.hallans.co.uk sits behind the IP allowlist (PORTAL_ALLOWED_IPS) and isn't reachable from a customer tapping a WhatsApp link, and a PDF doesn't get a link preview or open cleanly inside WhatsApp the way a normal webpage does. GitHub Pages is public with no login wall, and is what's already used to host pages like this one. Needs three environment variables set on this server (Render → your service → Environment): GITHUB_TOKEN a GitHub personal access token with Contents read/write on the Pages repo (classic token: "repo" scope; fine-grained token: Contents permission) GITHUB_OWNER the GitHub username/org that owns the Pages repo GITHUB_REPO the repo name Optional: GITHUB_BRANCH defaults to "main" GITHUB_PAGES_BASE_URL set this if the repo's Pages site is on a custom domain instead of the default https://.github.io/ Until those are set, this doesn't fail the request — it reports configured:false so the button falls back to downloading the HTML file for you to add to the repo by hand (drag-and-drop on github.com, or `git add` + `git commit` + `git push`) in the meantime. Each publish writes up to two files: the flyer page itself, and — only if a cover image was supplied — a plain JPEG/PNG file for it. WhatsApp's link-preview crawler fetches og:image over plain HTTP and can't read a data: URI, so the cover needs its own real file/URL for a WhatsApp preview to show a picture at all; every other image on the page (layout, department photos) stays embedded as a data: URI, which is fine since those are only ever viewed inside the page itself. No new database table needed — nothing here is stored in Oracle at all. --------------------------------------------------------- */ const GITHUB_TOKEN = process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN || ''; const GITHUB_OWNER = process.env.GITHUB_OWNER || ''; const GITHUB_REPO = process.env.GITHUB_REPO || ''; const GITHUB_BRANCH = process.env.GITHUB_BRANCH || 'main'; const GITHUB_PAGES_BASE_URL = (process.env.GITHUB_PAGES_BASE_URL || (GITHUB_OWNER && GITHUB_REPO ? `https://${GITHUB_OWNER}.github.io/${GITHUB_REPO}` : '') ).replace(/\/$/, ''); function githubPagesConfigured() { return !!(GITHUB_TOKEN && GITHUB_OWNER && GITHUB_REPO); } // Creates or updates one file in the Pages repo via GitHub's Contents API. // GitHub rejects an update with no SHA (it reads as "you're overwriting // something you haven't seen"), so the current SHA is fetched first — // a 404 there just means the file doesn't exist yet, which is fine. async function githubPutFile(filePath, contentBase64, commitMessage) { const url = `https://api.github.com/repos/${GITHUB_OWNER}/${GITHUB_REPO}/contents/${encodeURIComponent(filePath)}`; const headers = { 'Authorization': `Bearer ${GITHUB_TOKEN}`, 'Accept': 'application/vnd.github+json', 'User-Agent': 'hallan-picking-backend' }; let sha; const existing = await fetch(`${url}?ref=${encodeURIComponent(GITHUB_BRANCH)}`, { headers }); if (existing.ok) { sha = (await existing.json()).sha; } else if (existing.status !== 404) { throw new Error(`GitHub GET ${filePath} failed: ${existing.status} ${await existing.text()}`); } const put = await fetch(url, { method: 'PUT', headers: { ...headers, 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ message: commitMessage, content: contentBase64, branch: GITHUB_BRANCH, ...(sha ? { sha } : {}) }) }); if (!put.ok) { throw new Error(`GitHub PUT ${filePath} failed: ${put.status} ${await put.text()}`); } return put.json(); } // Swaps the content="" of one tag for a new value — // used to drop the real, publicly-reachable GitHub Pages URL into // og:url/og:image after upload, since only this server knows the final // URL (the browser doesn't know GITHUB_PAGES_BASE_URL). Leaves the HTML // untouched if that meta tag isn't present for some reason. function replaceMetaContent(html, property, newContent) { const re = new RegExp(`(]*property=["']${property}["'][^>]*content=)["'][^"']*["']`, 'i'); return re.test(html) ? html.replace(re, `$1"${newContent.replace(/"/g, '"')}"`) : html; } // Does the actual publish (used by both the immediate route below and the // scheduled runner further down). Throws on bad input (with .status set) // or on a GitHub API failure; returns { ok:false, configured:false, message } // when GitHub Pages just isn't set up yet — that's not an error, just // nothing to do. async function publishWholesaleFlyerWebpage({ mode, html, cover_image_base64 }) { if (mode !== 'whole' && mode !== 'veg') { const err = new Error("mode must be 'whole' or 'veg'"); err.status = 400; throw err; } if (!html) { const err = new Error('html is required'); err.status = 400; throw err; } if (!githubPagesConfigured()) { return { ok: false, configured: false, message: 'GitHub Pages publishing isn\'t set up on the server yet (set GITHUB_TOKEN / GITHUB_OWNER / GITHUB_REPO in Render\'s Environment tab). Downloaded the file instead so you can add it to the repo by hand for now.' }; } const pageFile = mode === 'whole' ? 'wholesale-flyer.html' : 'veg-flyer.html'; const pageUrl = `${GITHUB_PAGES_BASE_URL}/${pageFile}`; let coverUrl = null; if (cover_image_base64) { const m = /^data:image\/(png|jpe?g);base64,(.+)$/i.exec(cover_image_base64); if (m) { const ext = /png/i.test(m[1]) ? 'png' : 'jpg'; const coverFile = (mode === 'whole' ? 'wholesale-flyer-cover' : 'veg-flyer-cover') + '.' + ext; await githubPutFile(coverFile, m[2], `Update ${coverFile}`); coverUrl = `${GITHUB_PAGES_BASE_URL}/${coverFile}`; } } let finalHtml = replaceMetaContent(html, 'og:url', pageUrl); if (coverUrl) finalHtml = replaceMetaContent(finalHtml, 'og:image', coverUrl); await githubPutFile(pageFile, Buffer.from(finalHtml, 'utf8').toString('base64'), `Update ${pageFile}`); return { ok: true, configured: true, url: pageUrl, coverUrl }; } app.post('/api/wholesale/flyer/publish-webpage', async (req, res) => { try { const result = await publishWholesaleFlyerWebpage(req.body || {}); res.json(result); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(e.status || 500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); /* --------------------------------------------------------- WHOLESALE FLYER WEBPAGE — SCHEDULED PUBLISH Lets "Update Wholesale Flyer Webpage" / "Update Veg Flyer Website" be scheduled for a future date/time instead of only ever running from a click — e.g. queue Monday's price update on Friday afternoon so it goes live at 6am Monday without anyone needing to be at a screen. Same shape as the WHOLESALE_WA_SCHEDULES runner above: a table, a poll loop that claims due rows, and stale-RUNNING recovery on boot. Table created automatically on boot by ensureWholesaleFlyerWebpageScheduleTable(): CREATE TABLE WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES ( SCHEDULE_ID NUMBER GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY, RUN_AT TIMESTAMP NOT NULL, FLYER_MODE VARCHAR2(10) NOT NULL, -- WHOLE | VEG (column can't be called MODE — it's an Oracle reserved word) HTML CLOB NOT NULL, COVER_IMAGE_BASE64 CLOB, STATUS VARCHAR2(20) DEFAULT 'PENDING' NOT NULL, -- PENDING|RUNNING|DONE|FAILED|CANCELLED RESULT_URL VARCHAR2(500), LAST_RUN_AT TIMESTAMP, LAST_RESULT VARCHAR2(4000), CREATED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT SYSTIMESTAMP ); --------------------------------------------------------- */ const WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEB_SCHEDULE_POLL_MS = 60 * 1000; // how often to check for due schedules async function ensureWholesaleFlyerWebpageScheduleTable() { const statements = [ `CREATE TABLE WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES ( SCHEDULE_ID NUMBER GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY, RUN_AT TIMESTAMP NOT NULL, FLYER_MODE VARCHAR2(10) NOT NULL, HTML CLOB NOT NULL, COVER_IMAGE_BASE64 CLOB, STATUS VARCHAR2(20) DEFAULT 'PENDING' NOT NULL, RESULT_URL VARCHAR2(500), LAST_RUN_AT TIMESTAMP, LAST_RESULT VARCHAR2(4000), CREATED_AT TIMESTAMP DEFAULT SYSTIMESTAMP )`, `CREATE INDEX WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEB_SCHED_IX ON WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES (STATUS, RUN_AT)` ]; await withConnection(async conn => { for (const sql of statements) { try { await conn.execute(sql, {}, { autoCommit: true }); console.log('[wholesale-flyer-web-schedule] applied:', sql.trim().split(/\s+/).slice(0, 3).join(' ')); } catch (e) { // 955 = object already exists (made on an earlier boot) — expected. if (e.errorNum !== 955) console.error('[wholesale-flyer-web-schedule] setup error:', e.message); } } }); } // ---- Schedules: list / create / cancel ---- app.get('/api/wholesale/flyer/publish-webpage/schedule', async (req, res) => { try { const rows = await withConnection(async conn => { const result = await conn.execute( `SELECT SCHEDULE_ID, RUN_AT, FLYER_MODE AS "MODE", STATUS, RESULT_URL, LAST_RUN_AT, LAST_RESULT, CREATED_AT FROM WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES ORDER BY RUN_AT DESC` ); return lower(result.rows); }); res.json({ schedules: rows }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.post('/api/wholesale/flyer/publish-webpage/schedule', async (req, res) => { const { mode, html, cover_image_base64, run_at } = req.body || {}; if (mode !== 'whole' && mode !== 'veg') return res.status(400).json({ error: "mode must be 'whole' or 'veg'" }); if (!html) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'html is required' }); const runAtDate = run_at ? new Date(run_at) : null; if (!runAtDate || isNaN(runAtDate.getTime())) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'A valid run_at date/time is required' }); if (runAtDate.getTime() <= Date.now()) return res.status(400).json({ error: "run_at must be in the future — use Publish for an immediate update" }); try { const scheduleId = await withConnection(async conn => { const result = await conn.execute( `INSERT INTO WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES (RUN_AT, FLYER_MODE, HTML, COVER_IMAGE_BASE64) VALUES (:runAt, :mode, :html, :cover) RETURNING SCHEDULE_ID INTO :scheduleId`, { runAt: runAtDate, mode: mode.toUpperCase(), html, cover: cover_image_base64 || null, scheduleId: { type: oracledb.NUMBER, dir: oracledb.BIND_OUT } }, { autoCommit: true } ); return result.outBinds.scheduleId[0]; }); res.json({ ok: true, schedule_id: scheduleId }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.delete('/api/wholesale/flyer/publish-webpage/schedule/:id', async (req, res) => { const id = Number(req.params.id); if (!id) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'valid schedule id is required' }); try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `UPDATE WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES SET STATUS = 'CANCELLED' WHERE SCHEDULE_ID = :id AND STATUS = 'PENDING'`, { id }, { autoCommit: true } ) ); if (result.rowsAffected === 0) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Only a pending, not-yet-run schedule can be cancelled' }); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); app.delete('/api/wholesale/flyer/publish-webpage/schedule/:id/dismiss', async (req, res) => { const id = Number(req.params.id); if (!id) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'valid schedule id is required' }); try { const result = await withConnection(conn => conn.execute(`DELETE FROM WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES WHERE SCHEDULE_ID = :id`, { id }, { autoCommit: true }) ); if (result.rowsAffected === 0) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Schedule not found' }); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (e) { console.error(e); res.status(500).json({ error: e.message }); } }); async function runOneWholesaleFlyerWebpageSchedule(schedule) { let finalStatus = 'DONE'; let resultUrl = null; let resultMessage = 'Published successfully.'; try { const html = await clobToString(schedule.html); const cover = schedule.cover_image_base64 ? await clobToString(schedule.cover_image_base64) : null; const result = await publishWholesaleFlyerWebpage({ mode: String(schedule.mode || '').toLowerCase(), html, cover_image_base64: cover }); if (!result.ok) { finalStatus = 'FAILED'; resultMessage = result.message || 'Publishing is not configured on the server.'; } else { resultUrl = result.url; } } catch (e) { console.error('Scheduled wholesale flyer webpage publish failed', e); finalStatus = 'FAILED'; resultMessage = e.message || 'Publish failed'; } await withConnection(conn => conn.execute( `UPDATE WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES SET STATUS = :status, RESULT_URL = :url, LAST_RUN_AT = SYSTIMESTAMP, LAST_RESULT = :result WHERE SCHEDULE_ID = :id`, { status: finalStatus, url: resultUrl, result: resultMessage.slice(0, 4000), id: schedule.schedule_id }, { autoCommit: true } ) ); } let wholesaleFlyerWebScheduleRunning = false; async function pollWholesaleFlyerWebpageSchedules() { if (wholesaleFlyerWebScheduleRunning) return; // don't overlap if a previous tick is still mid-run wholesaleFlyerWebScheduleRunning = true; try { const due = await withConnection(async conn => { const result = await conn.execute( `SELECT SCHEDULE_ID, RUN_AT, FLYER_MODE AS "MODE", HTML, COVER_IMAGE_BASE64 FROM WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES WHERE STATUS = 'PENDING' AND RUN_AT <= SYSTIMESTAMP ORDER BY RUN_AT` ); return lower(result.rows); }); for (const schedule of due) { // Atomically claim it so a second overlapping tick/instance can't // publish the same schedule twice. const claimed = await withConnection(async conn => { const result = await conn.execute( `UPDATE WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES SET STATUS = 'RUNNING' WHERE SCHEDULE_ID = :id AND STATUS = 'PENDING'`, { id: schedule.schedule_id }, { autoCommit: true } ); return result.rowsAffected === 1; }); if (!claimed) continue; await runOneWholesaleFlyerWebpageSchedule(schedule); } } catch (e) { console.error('Wholesale flyer webpage schedule poll failed', e); } finally { wholesaleFlyerWebScheduleRunning = false; } } // A server restart / redeploy mid-publish leaves that row stuck on RUNNING // forever — close it off as FAILED with an explanatory note so it shows up // in the schedule list instead of silently vanishing. async function recoverStaleWholesaleFlyerWebpageRuns() { const note = 'Interrupted by a server restart while publishing — check the webpage and re-schedule or publish again if needed.'; await withConnection(async conn => { await conn.execute( `UPDATE WHOLESALE_FLYER_WEBPAGE_SCHEDULES SET STATUS = 'FAILED', LAST_RUN_AT = SYSTIMESTAMP, LAST_RESULT = :result WHERE STATUS = 'RUNNING'`, { result: note }, { autoCommit: true } ); }); } /* --------------------------------------------------------- WHOLESALE PRICING — SCHEDULED CSV DOWNLOAD + EMAIL NOTIFICATIONS Lets "Download CSV" / "Test Download CSV" be scheduled for a future date/time (once, daily, or weekly) instead of only ever running from a click. Since nobody's looking at the screen when a schedule fires, the result (queued / picked up by the till / imported / failed) is emailed instead of shown in the on-screen import-status modal. Saved email addresses (WHOLESALE_NOTIFY_EMAILS) persist so staff pick from a list instead of retyping addresses every time; they stay until someone deletes them.
